Group judgement

Risks

D4239th of 55

Layer 3 · Risks

Written last by the Investment Analyst for its group

The forecasts are predictable where it matters least and unpredictable where it matters most. The operating base (revenue within about two points, the margin within about half a point) is steady because it is diffuse, variable in cost and no longer exposed to a large Trust & Safety vertical. The earlier sets agree on it, and it relies only on strengths the company has shown. But the base does not grow. All of the EPS growth in the next 18 months comes from restructuring and interest falling, and after that from buybacks: three lines, two of them board decisions, the first of them to be announced on 2027-02-25. The cone for FY2027 clean EPS is about EUR 11.3 to 14.2 around EUR 13.1, skewed slightly down. The cone for the share price is several times wider, because the multiple is company-driven, has just risen by about 45% on a change of governance, and is hostage to dated events. Disclosure will not settle the two questions that decide the operating base (is the margin kept once the savings stop adding; does growth outside Trust & Safety hold near +0.5%) before late July and early November 2027.

Steer for the memo.

  1. Treat the forecast as a held operating base plus a financial bridge, and judge each separately. The base deserves moderate confidence. The bridge deserves confidence only after the board has stated its restructuring and capital plans.
  2. Use my changed inputs: FY2027 LFL about -1.0% (H1 about -2.3%, H2 about +0.3%), a recurring margin of about 14.3%, and no buybacks before FY2029. They put the base case near the middle of the cone.
  3. Expect negative revenue prints until Q3 2027, and do not read them as new deterioration unless growth excluding Trust & Safety falls below zero.
  4. On riskiness (the memo's topic 9): the case is simple to state and hard to monitor; its drivers are few but discretionary; its risks are sudden (a client programme, a reset, an AI launch) rather than gradual.
  5. On the model's return (topic 12): the earnings part is small and fairly reliable (about +6% to the 18-month column on my inputs), the dividend part is reliable (12.7%), and the multiple part is the whole question (D-04, the table in point 6).
C-79

How narrow is the business perimeter (number of countries and business lines), how is it changing, and what does that mean for predictability?

F3043rd of 55

Layer 1 · Company - Economic strength

Judgement. The perimeter is broad in geography and narrow in business lines: nearly 100 countries, 400 languages and more than 1,500 clients, in two segments of six to eight units. It is narrowing at the margins (exits, reclassification) and could narrow sharply in lines if Specialized Services is sold. A sale would not narrow the geography, and would remove the steadier, higher-margin earnings. The many hubs, currencies, labour regimes and client verticals make predictability low, and a single-segment Core Services group would be less predictable, not more.

D-02

To what extent does past and present fundamental delivery make the expected change plausible, and to what extent does the thesis rely only on strengths the business demonstrably has, rather than on strengths it would need to acquire?

C7419th of 55

Layer 3 · Risks

Judgement. The change in our forecast is small, and almost all of it rests on strengths the business has shown: scale and client diversification, cost execution, cash conversion, tax and financing. That is by construction. The two earlier sets built a base case that needs no pricing power, no retained AI productivity, no shift to outcome pricing at scale and no proprietary technology, which are the strengths the company lacks. The company's own 2028 targets, the sell-side bull case and any re-rating above the low end of the multiple range do rely on those missing strengths. So the base case is plausible, and the upside case is not yet plausible. Inside the base case two assumptions go beyond the demonstrated record: that the recurring margin holds within about 20 basis points while Core revenue is flat, for which no labour-based peer offers a precedent, and that restructuring falls and stays down.

Reasoning.

  1. What the base case needs in FY2027, set against what has been delivered.
What FY2027 needs Delivered? Evidence
Trust & Safety drag falling to about 1.4 points, and to nil by H2 2027 Arithmetic Needs only that the vertical stops falling from about EUR 90 million a quarter; Morgan Stanley says the remaining work is the sensitive kind that resists automation; Redburn expects it to keep falling into FY2027
Growth excluding Trust & Safety of about +0.5% Yes, with a shrinking margin of safety +1.7% in H1 2026 and +2.0% in Q3 (Core excluding Trust & Safety +2.9%); about zero to +1% implied for Q4
Recurring margin of about 14.3% Group yes, Core no Group 14.4-15.1% since 2021; Core 12.4% (FY2024), 11.9% (FY2025 restated), 10.5% in H1 2026 (down 50 basis points on H1 2025)
Savings of EUR 150-170 million delivered Yes Majorel synergies delivered; EUR 109 million of restructuring executed in H1 2026; H1 2026 margin 20 basis points above consensus
Restructuring down to EUR 80 million Once FY2025 (EUR 16 million) followed FY2024 (EUR 112 million); management says FY2027-FY2028 will be "materially lower" than FY2026
Tax below 30%; interest falling Yes 29.0% in H1 2026; average maturity 4.5 years
Net free cash flow of about EUR 750 million Yes EUR 1,084 million (FY2024), EUR 870 million (FY2025); 9.5% of sales over four years against 7% at Concentrix

  1. The strengths the base case does not use are the ones the evidence says are missing. - Pricing power in Core. Renewals at Concentrix carry 2-5% like-for-like price pressure, rebids 3-7%, competitive bids 10-20%. More than 80% of new deals and renewals carry an automation component. New programmes are 15-25% smaller than two years ago (channel checks, May to November 2026). - Retained productivity. Morgan Stanley records that management did not say how much of the savings stays in the margin. - Outcome pricing at scale. It is over 15% of revenue and growing mid-single-digit. In a Firstsource survey 76% of buyers say they are open to it and 5% of contracts use it; large clients prefer per-seat contracts for clarity (Ambit). - Technology that clients pay for. Clients build or choose their own. None of these appears in the inputs. That is why the forecast is more predictable than the business is attractive.

  2. The precedents separate two kinds of change, and only one of them is ours. The AlphaSense search found no labour-based customer-experience provider that expanded its margin from a cost programme while revenue was flat or falling. The providers that raised margins were growing: Genpact (revenue +7.3%, more than half of revenue no longer priced per head, gross margin up for 14 quarters), Firstsource (guiding 10.5-13% growth, over half of revenue outcome-based, eight quarters of margin gains), Ibex (+15.9%, offshore). The providers that shrank lost margin or solvency: TELUS Digital (EBITDA margin from 17.7% to 13.0% in a year), TTEC (revenue -11%, covenants amended), Foundever (recapitalised), Atento (default), Concentrix (synergies delivered, margin lower, USD 2.6 billion written off). Margin expansion came with growth, never with cost-cutting alone. Teleperformance's 15.5% target for 2028 is therefore implausible on demonstrated strengths, and a held margin is the most that the record supports. Teleperformance itself is the only precedent for holding it (14.4-15.1% while organic growth went from about +2% to about -2%), and it did so with help from Specialized Services mix that is now finite.

  3. The weakest demonstrated link is the Core margin. The group margin has been delivered; the Core margin has fallen every year since FY2024. The base case needs Core to stop falling at about 11.3-11.5%. The support is that the highest-margin loss (Trust & Safety) is nearly complete. The risk is that the same clients' automation moves into voice, which is 64% of interactions: global call-centre employment was falling 4.1% a year by mid-2026 (Morgan Stanley, Revelio data), faster than offshoring alone explains.

  4. The chief executive's profile fits the base case better than the bull case. The base case asks for decisions on cost, capital and the portfolio, which eight months of action have shown. It does not ask him to change the revenue outcome, which nothing has shown. The one test of forecasting failed (guide reconfirmed in July, cut in November). The precedents warn about a different risk: turnarounds in this industry failed when daily delivery slipped during restructuring (Atento's missed service levels; the loss of 90% of experienced leaders at IGT Solutions after its chief executive left). Teleperformance has lost its founder, two deputy chief executives and its finance director in two years, and has no chief operating officer.

  5. Specialized Services: the 33.0% margin is demonstrated, but part of it is borrowed. It rests on moving interpreters offshore (5,900 by end-2025) and on Core's delivery network. LanguageLine's volumes are rising, but its nearest listed comparable, AMN's language services, saw revenue fall 1% in FY2025 and 8% in Q1 2026 under price competition from Propio, which has bought Akorbi and CyraCom. I keep the second set's easing of the margin to 31-32%.

D-03In relation to the differences between the forecasts over the next 18 months to four years, vs. the last four to six years' financials, how diffuse or concentrated are the key drivers that generate the differences?D3837th of 55full answer under 2a Internal risks
D-04

How wide is the cone of plausible outcomes over the next 18 months, how skewed is it, and so how predictable is the case?

C-4526th of 55

Layer 3 · Risks

Judgement. The cone for the fundamentals is moderate in width and skewed down. The cone for the share price is wide, because the equity is a leveraged claim valued at a multiple that can halve, or rise by half, on the same earnings. For FY2026 almost nothing is open. For FY2027 I put LFL growth at -1.0% within a range of -3% to 0%, the recurring margin at 14.3% within 13.5% to 14.7%, and clean EPS at about EUR 13.1 within EUR 11.3 to 14.2, with a probability-weighted value of about EUR 12.8-13.0. After my changes the base case sits close to the middle of the cone; before them it sat in the upper half. The case is predictable enough to say what the business will earn to within about 10% either way, and not predictable enough to say what the market will pay for it.

Reasoning.

  1. FY2026 is nearly closed. Seven weeks remain. Q4 LFL is the only open operating item (about -3.0% in the inputs; brokers -1.7% to -2.9%; the guide allows -1.5% to about -5%). The margin guide was reaffirmed on 2026-11-05. Clean EPS of about EUR 12.5 is good to about 2% either way. What is open for FY2026 is not the result but what comes with it: the impairment test and the restructuring total.

  2. FY2027: the scenarios (my calculations on the model's arithmetic).

FY2027 Bear Base (my inputs) Upside Second set's bull
Probability 25% 50% 20% 5%
LFL growth -3% -1.0% 0% +1.5%
Recurring EBITA margin 13.5% 14.3% 14.7% 15.0%
Restructuring (EUR m) 120 80 50 30
Clean EPS (EUR) about 11.3 about 13.1 about 14.2 about 15.0
Against the base -14% +8% +15%

I give the second set's bull case only 5%. With a Trust & Safety drag of about 1.4 points for the year, and 2.5-3 points in the first half, growth of +1.5% for the year needs about +3% from the rest of the group, half as much again as the best quarter of 2026. The weighted EPS is about EUR 13.0. A step loss of one large client programme (3-5% of revenue; the second set's 15-25% probability in 18 months, which I accept) would take a further 7-13% off EPS, and brings the expected value to about EUR 12.8.

  1. Why the cone is not wider. - Costs are variable: a point of volume moves clean EPS by about 2.5%. - No client is above 6% of revenue. - Trust & Safety, the source of the 2026 surprise, is now under 4% of revenue. - Tax, interest, depreciation and capital expenditure are mechanical. - The margin has ended within about 50 basis points of the initial guide in recent years (FY2025: up to 15.1% guided, 14.6% delivered).

  2. Why it is skewed down. - The company's organic growth has ended below the midpoint of its initial guide in each of 2023 to 2026, by up to 3 points, and never above it. - Price is where the leverage is: a point of unmatched price is about 9.5% of clean EPS, and every 2026 data point on price moved against the company. - The restructuring line has been raised before. - The step-loss tail has no upside twin: no single client can add 3-5% of revenue in a quarter. - Peers show how far outcomes can spread in 18 months in this industry: revenue from -11% (TTEC) to +16% (Ibex), an EBITDA margin down 4.7 points in a year (TELUS Digital). Teleperformance sits at the stable end of that spread, but the spread is the industry's.

  3. The second half of 2027 holds most of the uncertainty. The first half is largely set by arithmetic: the Trust & Safety drag and the savings are both known. In the second half both drop out, and the result depends on the two things that cannot be observed in advance: growth excluding Trust & Safety, and whether the margin holds once the programme stops adding. The cone therefore narrows little before November 2027.

  4. The equity cone is several times wider than the earnings cone. Net debt is about half of a lease-inclusive enterprise value, so a 5% miss in EBITDA at a constant multiple is about 10% of market value. And the multiple has ranged from 3.3 to 27.6 times in five years. On the model's own measure (two-year-forward EPS at 2027-12-31, which is FY2029 EPS, about EUR 14.4 on my inputs, plus EUR 9.10 of dividends), my calculation of the return from EUR 71.68 is:

Multiple at 2027-12-31 3.5x (fair trough) 5.3x (today's, unchanged) 6x 7x 18.89x (guideline)
Value per share including dividends (EUR) about 60 about 85 about 96 about 110 about 282
Total return about -17% about +19% about +33% about +54% about +293%

The earnings cone moves these figures by about 10% either way; the multiple moves them by a factor of five. The dividend (EUR 9.10 over the period, 12.7% of the price) is the only part of the return that is nearly certain. It is covered about 2.8 times by free cash flow.

D-05

How helpful is the company's disclosure on operating momentum and key drivers of the thesis to monitor the case over about the next six months?

F4246th of 55

Layer 3 · Risks

Judgement. The disclosure is moderately helpful for the revenue engine and for the one decision that sets FY2027 EPS, and unhelpful for the margin and for price, which are the two things the thesis most needs to see. In the six months to mid-May 2027 the company will publish two revenue figures (Q4 2026 and Q1 2027), each with growth excluding Trust & Safety; one margin (H2 2026); and its FY2027 framework. That is enough to confirm FY2026, to learn the restructuring and buyback stance, and to track the engine outside Trust & Safety quarter by quarter. It is not enough to test whether savings are being kept, because the first margin that can show it (H1 2027) is published in late July 2027, and the first quarter that laps Trust & Safety is published in early November 2027. The next six months will tell us what management intends, not whether the forecast is right.

Reasoning.

  1. What is disclosed, and how often. - Quarterly: revenue by segment and region; LFL growth for the group and, since 2026, excluding Trust & Safety; the activity mix (Trust & Safety as a rounded share); counts of AI projects (over 1,400) and of clients using them (35%). - Half-yearly: recurring EBITA by segment; restructuring charged and non-recurring cash paid (EUR 56 million in H1 2026); the bridge from operating cash flow to net free cash flow, which AlphaSense judges more complete than peers'; the tax rate; net debt; working capital; any change to impairment assumptions (Spain at H1 2026). - Yearly: discount rates, terminal growth and sensitivities for each cash-generating unit; client concentration; currency exposures and the hedge book; factoring at the balance sheet date.

  2. What is not disclosed. Price against volume; the share of revenue up for renewal; contract length; the revenue of Trust & Safety in euros, of data services for AI and of each Specialized business; the margin on outcome-based work; the timetable of restructuring cash; headroom by cash-generating unit; any quarterly margin; EPS guidance.

  3. The calendar for the next six months, with what each event can show.

Date Event Reads on My reference points
About mid-January 2027 Concentrix Q4 FY2026 results and FY2027 guide Renewal pricing, offshoring, impairment practice Its stated aim of "stable to growing" margins with about 10% of revenue moving offshore
2027-02-25 (FactSet; the memo of 2026-10-11 gave 2027-02-18; unconfirmed) FY2026 results Q4 LFL and growth excluding Trust & Safety; H2 margin; FY2027 guide; restructuring guide; dividend; impairment; buyback stance Q4 LFL about -3.0%. Growth excluding Trust & Safety: +1.5% or better supports the base case, zero or below contradicts it. H2 recurring margin about 15.2%.   1. Readings from outside the company are at least as useful over six months. Concentrix and TaskUs report quarterly with margins. Channel checks on renewal pricing are frequent (more than five in the first twelve days of November 2026). Morgan Stanley publishes call-centre headcount from Revelio data (-4.1% a year at mid-2026; a slowing would be the first sign of stabilisation). The peso and rupee are quoted daily. Launches of consumer or enterprise AI agents are not scheduled, but have moved the shares 14-20% in a day.

  2. Why this matters for predictability. A half-yearly margin, in a business whose margin is the net of large opposing flows (D-03), means that a change in any one flow is seen up to eight months late. The July 2026 reconfirmation and the November 2026 cut happened inside one such blind interval. The same can happen between February and July 2027.

D-06

How clearly does what the company says it will achieve trace into specific operational drivers, onto identifiable revenue, cost and margin lines (the key fundamental milestones and measures), and through to earnings per share; and how clear is the external transmission from value-chain dynamics to earnings per share?

F4540th of 55

Layer 3 · Risks

Judgement. The trace is clear from the company's cost and capital promises to EPS, and broken from its revenue and margin promises to EPS. Savings, restructuring, tax, leverage and free cash flow each map onto a named line with a number and a date. Revenue growth and the margin do not: the company states outcomes (LFL growth, a 14.6% margin, 15.5% by 2028) without the drivers between them (price, volume, offshoring, retention of savings), gives no EPS guidance, and has no single EPS definition that brokers share. The external transmission is clear in direction and in three measurable channels (Trust & Safety, offshoring, currency), and opaque in the one that matters most, price at renewal. An analyst can therefore build the bottom half of the income statement from the company's words and must build the top half without them.

Reasoning.

  1. Promises that trace cleanly.
What the company says Driver Line To EPS (my calculation)
EUR 150-170 million of run-rate savings, half in H2 2026, the rest in 2027 About 5,400 employees affected in H1 2026; overheads; recruitment automation Personnel and external costs within recurring EBITA Gross, about EUR 1.8-2.0 a share if all were kept; the company does not say how much is
Restructuring at the high end of EUR 120-140 million; "materially lower" in FY2027-FY2028 Severance, France, site closures Non-recurring items; cash with a lag EUR 10 million is EUR 0.12 of clean EPS; nil on adjusted EPS
Tax rate below 30% Merging Majorel entities Tax One point is about 1.5% of EPS
Leverage to 1.2 times by 2028 Free cash flow less the dividend Net debt; net financial items EUR 100 million of debt repaid is about EUR 0.05
Adjusted free cash flow of EUR 800-850 million Before restructuring cash The company's bridge About EUR 700-740 million after it

  1. Promises that do not trace. - "LFL growth of -1.5% to -2.5%" and "4-6% by 2028". No split between Trust & Safety, price, offshoring and volume is given. The size of Trust & Safety comes from brokers. The split of the November cut (half Trust & Safety, a quarter offshoring, a quarter Specialized Services and healthcare volumes) comes from Morgan Stanley's account of management's remarks. - "A margin of 14.6%" with the savings target raised. The bridge from savings to margin is missing. Deutsche Bank's question, why EUR 30 million of extra savings in 2026 left the guide unchanged, has had no numerical answer. - "Outcome-based pricing is accretive" (over 15% of revenue). No margin, no experience of penalties and no working-capital effect are disclosed; Ambit warns that such contracts need upfront investment and lengthen receivables. - "AI as an opportunity" (over 1,400 projects; an AI solution in over 70% of new deals, per Morgan Stanley). These are measures of activity with no revenue or margin attached. - EPS. None is guided. The company's adjusted net profit adds back acquired amortisation, synergy and reorganisation costs; brokers follow it or not, so the "consensus" EPS is a blend (second set). The chief executive's bonus is partly paid on AI adoption (20%), and the long-term plan on free cash flow above EUR 2.3 billion. Neither is an EPS measure.

  2. The external transmission, channel by channel. - Client automation of a vertical, to volume: clear, and now measured. Trust & Safety's share and the growth rate with and without it are published. One point of group revenue lost in a high-margin vertical is about 2.5-3.5% of recurring EBITA (my estimate). - Offshoring, to revenue and margin: clear in direction. About -150 basis points a year of growth (Morgan Stanley), with the margin percentage stable or slightly up; Concentrix says the same. - Currency, to revenue and cost: clear and quantified. One cent of USD/EUR is about EUR 20 million of revenue; a 10% move in the peso is 3.6% of EPS and in the rupee 3.0%; hedges cover about twelve months. - Renewal pricing, to margin: opaque. This is the largest channel (one point of unmatched price is about 7% of recurring EBITA and 9.5% of clean EPS), and the only evidence is from channel checks on a competitor. - Hub wages and statute, to cost: clear in direction, unquantified in pass-through (Colombia, Metro Manila, India's Labour Codes). - Clients' technology choices, to the role of the outsourcer: slow, and unobservable until a contract changes.

  3. What this means. The lines that trace cleanly are the lines that produce the forecast's EPS growth (D-03). That helps: the growth can be audited against stated commitments. The lines that do not trace are the ones where the forecast could be wrong by most. Company statements about revenue and margin should carry little weight in the memo unless they come with a bridge, and the February framework should be judged first on whether it supplies one.

Judgement. The company has not upgraded revenue guidance in years. The pattern is an optimistic initial guide, held or "reconfirmed" through mid-year, then cut, with the cut explained by external or isolated causes. The new chief executive's first cycle followed the same pattern: reconfirmed in July, cut in November. Morgan Stanley's June 2026 hope of a "conservative, safe-sided guidance to establish a beat-and-raise cycle" did not materialise. This is Bernstein's "over-promising management archetype", the opposite of Rational's sandbagging.

C-69

What is the company's record of missing, meeting or beating guidance and sell-side estimates over the last one, three and five years, including in comparable environmental conditions?

F2553rd of 55

Layer 1 · Sell-side estimates

Judgement. It meets or beats on what it controls (margins, cash, overheads) and misses on revenue. Over one year: a mixed quarterly record and a missed annual organic guide. Over three years: serial revenue misses and about 35% cut from FY2026 EPS. Over five years: strong in 2021-2022, weak since. No comparable disruption regime exists; in the 2009 downturn (organic -1.1%) it outperformed traditional providers.

Reasoning.

  1. One year. - Q1 2026: -2.2% against -2.0% consensus (a miss). - Q2: -1.2% against -1.4% (a beat). - H1: margin +20 basis points and EPS +6% (beats); revenue +0.7% against consensus. - Q3: a miss, and the FY2026 organic guide cut.
  2. Three years. The 2023 target was cut from 10% to 6%. FY2024 was cut to 2%. In FY2025 like-for-like targets were cut twice and organic growth ended at 1.0%. Free cash flow guidance was lowered in late 2025. EPS was cut 35% over three years.
  3. Five years. Growth booms in 2020-2021 (+11.6% organic in 2020). Since 2023, persistent downward revisions for "almost three consecutive years" (Bernstein).
P-06

After assessing the track record of delivery in greater detail, how much weight should the forecasts for the next 18 months to four years carry, and in which direction should they be leaned?

F3052nd of 55

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Judgement. The record splits by line, and so should the weight. On revenue the record is four consecutive years of cuts, including the new chief executive's first guide; revenue forecasts, mine included, deserve low weight and should be leaned down. On the recurring margin the record is mostly delivered within about 50 basis points; margin forecasts deserve moderate weight for FY2026-FY2027, leaned slightly down beyond. On tax, interest and capital expenditure the record is good and the drivers are mechanical; these deserve high weight, leaned neither way, or slightly up on tax. On restructuring the record is of envelopes raised; forecasts of falling restructuring deserve low weight and should be leaned up (more cost). Overall: for the next 18 months the forecasts deserve moderate weight with a downside lean on revenue and clean EPS of about 2-4%; for 2028-2030 they deserve low weight, with a downside lean on revenue and an uncertain lean on EPS, because buybacks could offset the operating risk.

Reasoning.

  1. Revenue: a consistent miss. The 2023 pro forma target was cut from 10% to 6%; FY2024 ended near 2%; FY2025 was cut twice and ended at +1.0%; FY2026 went from 0% to +2% to -1.5% to -2.5% (2026-11-05), after a July reconfirmation that needed a sharp H2 acceleration. Quarterly against consensus in 2026: Q1 -2.2% against -2.0% (miss), Q2 -1.2% against -1.4% (beat), Q3 a miss. The causes were each presented as specific (a visa contract, immigration policy, the Colombian peso, Trust & Safety), but they recur. Bernstein calls management "over-promisers who regularly downgrade targets".
  2. Margin: mostly delivered. The recurring margin has stayed within about 14.4-15.1% since 2021 despite the revenue misses; H1 2026 beat consensus by 20 basis points (13.6% against 13.3%). The core margin did not hold (12.4% to 10.5%); the group margin was held by Specialized mix and programmes, which are finite.
  3. Cash: delivered, then guided lower. FY2024 net free cash flow of EUR 1,084 million beat consensus. FY2025 guidance was lowered in late 2025. The FY2026 guide is EUR 800-850 million adjusted (about EUR 690-740 million after restructuring cash, my estimate). Cash conversion and working capital (17-17.3% of revenue) have been reliable.
  4. Costs and synergies: delivered in substance. Majorel synergies were EUR 94 million in 2024, and the company said in July 2024 it was on track for EUR 150 million run-rate by end-2025; the sources also cite "EUR 20-30 million more" in 2025. The first set read this as 75-85% delivery. I read it as ambiguous between in-year income-statement savings and a run-rate target, so it does not establish a miss. Restructuring itself was executed quickly in 2026 (EUR 109 million in H1).
  5. Restructuring and "one-offs": envelopes raised. EUR 70-90 million became EUR 120-140 million within a year, at the high end; charges recur most years.
  6. The sell-side's own record on this company. FactSet mean EPS for FY2026 is down 9.6% over twelve months, FY2027 -12.7%, FY2028 -23.0%, and still falling (FY2027 -2.3% over one month). Estimates have been cut by about 35% for FY2026 over three years. A forecast that sits on consensus has historically been too high.
  7. Weight and lean, by line.
Line Record Weight, next 18 months Weight, 2028-2030 Lean on my base case
Organic revenue Missed four years running Low-moderate (cone about ±2 points) Low Down (about -0.5 points a year)

  1. Why I do not lean the base case further down. My base case already sits below consensus on revenue (about -0.9% in FY2027-FY2028) and keeps restructuring that brokers drop. The first set's steer, to accept management's cost and cash numbers and haircut its revenue and margin ambitions, is the right rule, and my base case applies it. Leaning further would double-count.
  2. A new credibility test is coming. The reset on 2027-02-25 is the first framework from the new chief executive. If it sets FY2027 LFL guidance at or below about -1% to +1% and restructuring at or below EUR 50-80 million, the revenue lean should be reduced; a guide at or above +2% would repeat the pattern and should be discounted.

For the next Analyst. Weight the FY2026 forecast highly (seven weeks of the year remain; Q4 LFL is the only open item, at about -3% in my base case against -1.5% to -5.5% implied by the guide range). Weight FY2027 moderately, and lean revenue and clean EPS down by about 2-4% in the risk analysis. Treat 2028-2030 as a range, not a point.

Growth rate

Group judgement

Financial forecasts

F3245th of 55

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Written last by the Investment Analyst for its group

The forecasts describe a business that has stopped growing and will not start again on its own: revenue flat to 2027 and about +1% a year after, recurring EBITA flat at about EUR 1.43-1.46 billion, and free cash flow of EUR 730-800 million, all below the FY2024-FY2025 levels. The operating line is held flat by a treadmill (price given back at each renewal, savings programmes to replace it, restructuring to pay for them), and operating leverage is too low to help when revenue is flat. The EPS growth that remains (about 7% a year clean, about 4% adjusted) comes from below operating profit: restructuring falling, interest falling, and buybacks from 2028. Those drivers are more predictable than the operating ones but not certain: restructuring has been raised before, and buybacks depend on cash and on the board.

Steer for the third and fourth sets.

  1. Predictability. For the next 18 months the forecast rests on two drivers: group growth excluding Trust & Safety (about +1%) and the restructuring line (EUR 80 million in FY2027). Revenue deserves low-to-moderate confidence with a downside skew; the margin moderate confidence; tax, interest and capital expenditure high confidence. The FY2027 clean EPS cone is about EUR 11.3 to 15.0 around a base of EUR 13.46, weighted slightly down (about EUR 13.2).
  2. Guidance. The reset on 2027-02-25 is likely to lower the 2028 targets (4-6% LFL growth and about 15.5% margin) towards about 0-2% and 14.5-15%. My base case is the kind of framework that would be believable; a new guide above it would repeat the four-year pattern of cuts.
  3. Consensus. EBITDA consensus is about right on my numbers. Revenue consensus is about 1% too high for FY2027-FY2028. FY2027-FY2028 free cash flow consensus is about 12-14% too high unless restructuring stops. EPS consensus is a blend of definitions, so revisions must be read broker by broker: brokers that deduct restructuring are close to my clean figures; those that exclude it are close to my adjusted figures.
  4. Narrative. The market's "cheap at under five times" depends on the adjusted basis and on restructuring being temporary; the clean basis raises the multiple by about 13%, and a goodwill impairment on 2027-02-25 would feed the "value destroyed" story without changing cash.

P-08

After assessing below-operating-profit cost items (e.g. tax, interest, one-offs, minority investments, etc.) and share buybacks or issuance in greater detail, how is it likely to shape earnings per share, net income and free cash flow over the next 18 months to four years, versus the last four to six years?

B+705th of 55

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Judgement. Below operating profit is where almost all of the EPS growth in my forecast comes from. Clean EPS rises from EUR 12.44 (FY2026) to EUR 16.14 (FY2030), about +6.7% a year, while recurring EBITA grows about 0.5% a year. Of the roughly EUR 3.70 a share of growth: falling restructuring contributes about EUR 1.2, lower interest about EUR 0.7, buybacks (net of share issuance) about EUR 1.4, and operating profit only about EUR 0.4 (my attribution, on FY2030 shares). That is the reverse of 2021-2024, when EPS rose on revenue and acquisitions. Free cash flow stays at EUR 730-800 million, below FY2024's EUR 1,084 million and FY2025's about EUR 870 million in every year, because the EBITDA is lower and restructuring persists. Two below-the-line items are large risks rather than drivers: restructuring above my run-rate, and a goodwill impairment that would hit reported EPS and the narrative but not cash.

Reasoning.

  1. Tax: a genuine, mostly delivered gain. The reported rate was 39.8% (FY2024) and 36.8% (FY2025), inflated by non-deductible items such as impairments; the adjusted rate fell from 33.1% (H1 2025) to 29.0% (H1 2026) as Majorel legal entities merged, and is guided below 30% for FY2026. I use 29.5% in every year, against Bernstein's 30.0% flat. Pillar Two added EUR 4 million in H1 2026. Peers run lower (Concentrix about 24%, TaskUs 26.1%), so the rate is not low by sector standards; the new finance director's background in transfer pricing is a modest positive. Each point of tax rate is about EUR 11-12 million of net income, about 1.5% of EPS. Risk: a rebound towards 33% if entity mergers stall or audits go against the company (Bernstein's downside: 36%).
  2. Interest: falling, slowly. EUR 282 million (FY2025, FactSet, including leases and other financial costs) to EUR 240 million (FY2026), then EUR 220 million, 205 million, 195 million and 185 million. The higher coupon on the 2026 bond and buybacks from 2028 slow the fall (P-02).
  3. Restructuring: the largest swing item, and the one most likely to differ from consensus. I carry EUR 140 million (FY2026), 80 million (FY2027), 60 million (FY2028) and 50 million a year after. History: about EUR 128 million, 112-119 million, 30 million and 140 million (FY2023-FY2026). Brokers that publish an adjusted EPS excluding restructuring are unaffected by it; those that deduct it (and the clean EPS) are. A new programme with the reset on 2027-02-25 is the risk (P-03).
  4. Amortisation of acquired intangibles and impairment: excluded from adjusted and clean EPS, not from reported. EUR 218 million (FY2025), EUR 106 million (H1 2026); I assume about EUR 210 million (FY2026), falling about EUR 10 million a year as Majorel and older intangibles amortise. After an assumed tax effect of about 25%, reported net income is about EUR 160 million below clean net income: about EUR 565 million (FY2026, reported basic EPS about EUR 9.7) and about EUR 640 million (FY2027, about EUR 10.9), against EUR 497 million and EUR 8.48 in FY2025. Any FY2026 goodwill impairment comes off that reported figure.
  5. One-offs in the cash flow. EUR 110 million of receivables were factored at end-2025; if the facility is not renewed at end-2026, working capital absorbs it once. Restructuring cash lags charges (the French liabilities were not all recognised in H1 2026). I carry about EUR 30 million a year of cash costs that the model's inputs do not capture, which is why my free cash flow cross-check is about EUR 30 million below the model's mechanical figure.
  6. Minority interests, associates and minority investments: immaterial. FactSet shows no minorities and no associates. The AI stakes (EUR 25.9 million in Sanas, Ema and Parloa; up to EUR 100 million budgeted) appear in my acquisitions line at EUR 25-40 million a year; they earn nothing in the income statement.

Its two weakest assumptions are that Core growth excluding Trust & Safety holds near +1% while discounting rises, and that restructuring falls to EUR 50-80 million a year.

4. The key judgements behind the base case, and how they connect to the first set.

  • Revenue in two engines, as the first set steered: Trust & Safety falling about 30-40% in 2026 and 25-30% in 2027 on a shrinking weight; the rest of the group at about +1% to +1.5%, held down by price and offshoring and lifted by vendor consolidation, back office and outcome-based work. I am about 0.5 points below the first set for FY2027, for the reason in P-04.
  • Margin as a treadmill: one third of the 2027 savings retained; Specialized easing from 33% towards 31%; Core recovering only slightly as Trust & Safety's drag ends.
  • Restructuring as a semi-recurring line, not zero: EUR 80 million in FY2027 is my central estimate for a new chief executive resetting the plan on 2027-02-25.
  • Below the line: tax at 29.5%; interest falling slowly; no buybacks until 2028 because, on my cash flows, leverage reaches the 1.2-1.5 times zone only in 2028.
  • The dividend held at EUR 4.50 for FY2026: the board's "de-leverage focus" and a new framework argue against a rise; cover is about 2.8 times free cash flow, so a cut is unlikely.
  • The disposal is outside the base case (P-08 gives the scenario).

5. What would move the base case, and the dated tests.

  • 2027-02-25 (FY2026 results and the reset): Q4 2026 LFL (base about -3.0%; -2% or better is upside), the FY2027 guide (my base sits at about -0.6% LFL), the restructuring guide (base EUR 80 million), the dividend (base EUR 4.50), the goodwill test, and any buyback timetable.
  • Late April 2027 (Q1 2027 revenue): LFL about -2.0% in my base; growth excluding Trust & Safety of at least +1%.
  • Late July 2027 (H1 2027 results): recurring margin about 13.9% in my base; 14.2% or more would show savings being retained; 13.6% or less would show none.
  • Throughout: the share of deals with upfront discounts, contract length at renewal, the outcome-based share of revenue (over 15% now), and any single-client programme loss.

7b

Margins/growth vs. past

C-53How high are gross and operating profit margins, and how are they trending?D4231st of 55full answer under 4c Financial profile
C-78

Beyond cutting costs, how much plausible scope is there to improve the financial outputs over the mid term; do peer precedents show it is achievable; would it come from a few big areas or many small ones; and is it congruent with the stated strategy?

F5050th of 55

Layer 1 · Company - Economic strength

Judgement. There is real scope to raise EPS beyond cost cuts, worth about 3-5% a year, but almost all of it lies below the operating line. It comes from several medium-sized, mechanical areas rather than one big one. It is congruent with the strategy. Peer precedents say margin expansion from mix and outcome-based pricing in this industry is unproven, while gains from tax, interest and buybacks are routine.

Reasoning.

  1. Tax. The rate is falling from about 33% (adjusted) to about 29-30%: about +5-6% to EPS, largely in FY2026.
  2. Interest. From deleveraging, +4-6% by FY2028 (C-76).
  3. Buybacks (my calculation). Each EUR 100 million at about EUR 71 retires about 1.4 million shares (about 2.3%), about +1.7% to EPS net of lost interest. Not before late 2027 on the current priority.
  4. Mix. Specialized Services (33%), digital channels (15-25% against 8-14% for voice), and offshoring (margin-accretive) each add tens of basis points.
  5. Outcome-based pricing. Claimed margins above 60% for agentic resolution (a single expert). Unproven at scale.
  6. The disposal. EPS-neutral at 5-8 times EBITA, accretive above about 8-10 times (C-15).
  7. Peer precedents. Pernod Ricard's EUR 900 million programme raised the operating margin 64 basis points while the gross margin fell 216 basis points: self-help defends the margin, it does not expand it. Concentrix has not expanded its margin. TaskUs's 15.9% rests on a specialised, technology-led mix.
C-80

To what extent has the core growth engine decelerated over the last twelve months: is the revenue growth rate running below the company's own normal rate, and is the input change force itself slowing?

F1554th of 55

Layer 1 · Risks

Judgement. The engine has decelerated deeply. Growth is about ten points below the company's normal rate, and the forces driving the slowdown are speeding up, not slowing. The only near-term relief is arithmetic (the Trust & Safety base effect), not a slowing of the forces.

Reasoning.

  1. Rates. The normal rate was 8.0% organic a year from 2010 to 2019. The last twelve months: FY2025 +1.0%; Q1 2026 -2.2%, Q2 -1.2%, Q3 -1.2%; Q4 implied about -1.5% to -5.5%. The FY2026 guide is -1.5% to -2.5%.
  2. The input forces are accelerating. - Trust & Safety fell from 8.0% to 5.5% of revenue within nine months. - The share of deals with upfront discounting rose from 10-15% to 15-25%. - Muse launched on 2026-09-08. - Morgan Stanley attributes the November cut to "worsening trends in trust and safety alongside accelerated offshoring".
  3. Peers. Concentrix revenue -1.2% in Q3 2026 and Telus Digital -7%. TaskUs is growing.

After assessing the other questions/items in the set in greater detail, what is the net likely impact to the main financial line items over the next 18 months to four years, versus the last four to six years?

F2847th of 55

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Judgement. The net impact is a change of regime, from a company that grew revenue at about 11% a year (2019-2025, mostly by acquisition) and EPS through operating profit, to one whose revenue is flat to 2027 and grows about 1% a year after, whose recurring EBITA is flat in money terms at about EUR 1.43-1.46 billion, and whose EPS grows about 7% a year on the clean basis (about 4% on the adjusted basis) only because restructuring, interest and the share count fall. Free cash flow settles about 10-30% below the FY2024-FY2025 level and the dividend grows about 3% a year instead of about 11%. The quality of the EPS growth is therefore low: it is mechanical and below the line, and the operating line beneath it has no tailwind. The cone is skewed down on revenue and margin and partly cushioned on EPS by buybacks.

Forecasts and upgrades

8

Probably upgrades this financial year to

–not yet rated

Memo topic 7, : the Rater's score

Behind the rating as a whole

Group judgement

The fourth set as a whole

F2550th of 55

Layer 4 · The fourth set as a whole

Written last by the Investment Analyst for its group

Our assessment is, for the most part, already reflected. Guidance for FY2026 matches it. The 2028 targets are far above it and are known to be going. Consensus is close to it on EBITDA, above it on FY2027 revenue, on free cash flow and on EPS after restructuring, and is still being cut. The sell-side narrative contains nearly every dynamic the first two sets found, at a share price in the bottom percentile of its own history. The memo's fifth test (guidance, estimates and expectations that do not capture the probable developments of the next 6 months and of months 6 to 18) is therefore not met in the favourable sense. For the next six months the developments that are not captured are negative: a first half of 2027 at about -2.3% LFL; a margin that does not rise in the first half and loses the savings contribution in the second; a free cash flow consensus about 15% too high; a reset that may come in two steps; and an impairment test at a share price within a few euros of book value. For months 6 to 18 the balance improves: the base effect lifts growth by about 2.5 points between the halves of 2027, the restructuring line falls, and a guide that is met once would be new. Beyond 18 months the price already discounts more decline than our base case contains, but that is a statement about value, not about news to come.

Steer for the memo.

  1. Guidance and estimates (topics 4, 7 and 8). There is no record of conservative guidance since 2022, no momentum in upgrades (sell-side momentum F, buy share 20%, revision score -11), and no scope for upgrades before the H1 2027 results at the earliest. Technical momentum is A, but it was earned on a change of governance, not on estimates, and the two ratings cannot both persist.
  2. Lifecycle (topic 10). The fundamentals are bottoming and not yet turning up: estimates are still falling, and the two confirming data points are dated late July and early November 2027. The share price turned up earlier, in February 2026, and stalled on 2026-11-05. I would describe the stock as bottoming with a failed first turn, not as turning up.
  3. The fair multiple (topic 11). The guideline's 18.89 times lies between the median (16.4 times) and the 75th percentile (23.9 times) of the ten-year range, which was set when the company grew 8% a year organically. My view, with the first set, is 5-7 times two-year-forward earnings until two quarters of positive organic growth have been reported, and the lower half of that range while estimates are falling. The business has no organic EPS growth, an earnings base that is 10-13% lower once restructuring is counted, leverage near 1.8 times, and a disruption debate that even its bulls do not dismiss. A higher multiple becomes arguable only after late 2027, and then perhaps 8 times, the tenth percentile of the ten-year range.
  4. The return (topic 12). At the guideline multiple the model shows +318% to 2027-12-31, of which earnings are 8 points and dividends 13. On my inputs the earnings part is about 6 points. The return that the forecasts themselves support is the dividend plus earnings growth: about 19% over that period at an unchanged multiple, with about +33% at 6 times and about -17% at 3.5 times (D-04). The memo should present the model's figure as the outcome of the guideline, and state the range above as the analyst's view of what drives it.
  5. What would change this judgement. Favourably: an FY2027 LFL guide with a midpoint at or below -1%; restructuring guided at EUR 50 million or less; growth excluding Trust & Safety at +1.5% or better in Q4 2026 and Q1 2027; an H1 2027 recurring margin of 14.0% or better; or a signed disposal of TLScontact or LanguageLine at a stated multiple with a stated use of proceeds. Unfavourably: a guide that again needs a second-half acceleration; a new restructuring programme above EUR 120 million; growth excluding Trust & Safety at or below zero; the loss of a large client programme; or an impairment large enough to bring the equity covenant into discussion.

I-01

How likely is company guidance to be raised, met or reduced at the next few earnings events (all such events over the next 18 months), and what would drive each outcome? Secondarily, how likely is any guidance, mid-term target or ambition for 18 months to three years out to be raised, met or reduced?

F2253rd of 55

Layer 4 · Guidance

Judgement. Over the next 18 months guidance is more likely to be met or reduced than raised, and the mid-term targets are near certain to be reduced. The FY2026 guide should be met. The FY2027 guide, due on 2027-02-25, is likely to be set at or a little below where consensus then stands on revenue, and at consensus on margin. I put the odds that it survives the year without a cut at a little better than even, the odds of a cut at about 35%, and of a raise at about 10%. The 2028 targets (4-6% growth, about 15.5% margin, EUR 3 billion of cumulative free cash flow) will be withdrawn or cut hard, possibly at a separate strategic update later in the first half of 2027. What would make this favourable is not a raise, which the arithmetic of 2027 rules out before the autumn, but a first guide that is then met. That would be new for this company in recent years, and new for this chief executive.

Reasoning.

  1. The events, with my probabilities.
Event Guidance at stake Raised or beaten / met / reduced or missed What drives each outcome
2027-02-25, FY2026 results FY2026: LFL -1.5% to -2.5%; margin 14.6% at constant currency; adjusted free cash flow EUR 800-850 million; restructuring EUR 120-140 million LFL 10 / 80 / 10. Margin 10 / 75 / 15. Cash flow 10 / 75 / 15. Restructuring above EUR 140 million: 25 A miss on LFL needs a Q4 below about -5%; brokers are at -1.7% to -2.9%. The margin was reaffirmed with seven weeks left. Cash flow depends on second-half collections (about 70% of the year). Restructuring is "at the high end" with French negotiations unfinished
2027-02-25 The first FY2027 guide See point 2
First half of 2027 (the same day, or a later strategic update) The 2028 targets 0 / 10 / 90 Bernstein models +1.3% growth and a 14.6% margin for FY2028, Morgan Stanley 14.1%. Management reiterated the targets in June 2026 and has since said it will revisit them
Late April 2027, Q1 revenue FY2027 guide 0 / 90 / 10 Too early to change; a Q1 near -2.5% is in the arithmetic
Late July 2027, H1 results FY2027 guide 5 / 70 / 25 The first margin with the full savings, and the weakest revenue half; the usual place for a reconfirmation that leans on the second half
Early November 2027, Q3 revenue FY2027 guide 10 / 65 / 25 The first quarter that laps Trust & Safety; also the quarter in which the 2025 and 2026 guides were cut
February 2028, FY2027 results FY2027 outcome; FY2028 guide Outcome within the initial range: about 60 As above

  1. Where the FY2027 guide is likely to land. My expectation: LFL growth in a range of about -2% to 0%; a recurring margin "stable" at about 14.5% at constant currency; restructuring "materially lower", with a figure of EUR 50-80 million; adjusted free cash flow of about EUR 750-850 million; and no buyback. Against consensus that is below on revenue (the FactSet median is +0.7% reported; five of ten brokers are still at +0.8% to +2.0% and a sixth at +0.6%), in line on margin and EBITDA, and below on free cash flow for the brokers that assume restructuring cash stops (Bernstein EUR 913 million, Redburn EUR 879 million). Against my inputs it would be in line on revenue and about 0.2 point above on margin.

  2. Why I expect a more cautious first guide than in the past, and why I do not rely on it. - For caution. The chief executive and the finance director have just been embarrassed by a guide they inherited in February, reconfirmed in July and cut in November. Morgan Stanley's notes make the cost to credibility explicit. A new framework is the natural moment to clear the decks, and a new chief executive has every reason to set a base he can beat. The company did guide conservatively and beat its initial objectives every year from 2018 to 2022 (Morgan Stanley), so the habit of cutting is four years old, not permanent. - Against. Four initial guides in a row (2023 to 2026) were too high, and each was cut in the second half. Former executives describe the commercial organisation as over-promising. The first half of 2027 will be about -2.3% on my arithmetic, so any full-year range whose midpoint is above about -1% is again a bet on the second half. And growth outside Trust & Safety is slowing as the guide is being written. - The test is simple and can be applied on the day. If the midpoint of the FY2027 LFL range is at or below about -1%, and it does not depend on a second-half acceleration beyond the Trust & Safety base effect, the guide is conservative. If the midpoint is around zero or above, the pattern is repeating.

  3. The restructuring guide is the one that moves earnings. Management has said that FY2027 and FY2028 will be "materially lower" than FY2026, and that the efficiency programme extends next into training, finance and support functions. Morgan Stanley models EUR 50 million a year; Redburn none; my input is EUR 80 million. I put a new programme of more than EUR 120 million at about 15%. That is lower than the first set implied, because the company-labelled record shows low years after programme years. It is higher than zero, because a strategic update by a new chief executive is when programmes are launched.

  4. The mid-term frame (18 months to three years). Expect it to be rebuilt around what the company controls: EBITA or EPS growth, free cash flow and leverage, as Morgan Stanley anticipates, with organic growth cut to low single digits and the margin to about the current level. A target of that kind has a fair chance of being met (I put it at about 55%), because it will rest on the same below-the-line levers as our forecast. The leverage target of 1.2 times by 2028 is likely to be kept and, on our cash flows, missed by a year unless a disposal is made. The EUR 3 billion cash-flow target (our cumulative figure is about EUR 2.24 billion) will be cut or dropped. A disposal would change every one of these numbers, and is the reason the frame may be delayed beyond February.

  5. Whether guidance is conservative against our assessment. FY2026: aligned with ours. FY2027: not yet given; likely near ours. 2028: far above ours and known to be going. There is no conservative guidance here that our forecast would beat. The most that can be said is that the downside from guidance is now mostly known and dated.

How likely are sell-side consensus forecasts, and the net breadth of revisions, to move higher or lower over the next few earnings events (over the next 18 months): to be upgraded, cut or held, and why? Secondarily, how likely are the consensus forecasts for 18 months to three years out to be upgraded, cut or held?

F2543rd of 55

Layer 4 · Sell-side estimates

Judgement. Consensus is more likely to fall than to rise at each event to the end of July 2027, and the breadth of revisions is likely to stay negative until then. The cuts still to come are modest for EBITDA (about 1-3%), about 1.3% for FY2027-FY2028 revenue, and largest for free cash flow (about 14-16%) and for EPS as stated by brokers that deduct restructuring (about 4-5%). Brokers that exclude restructuring from EPS are already close to our figures. The trough in estimates is most likely between the February reset and the H1 2027 results. A sustained cycle of upgrades needs the third quarter of 2027, and is more a 2028 event. For FY2028-FY2029 consensus is likely to be cut a little further on margin and then held, with buybacks, which no consensus line yet contains, as the eventual source of upgrades.

Reasoning.

  1. Where consensus stands against my inputs (FactSet medians of 2026-11-11; my calculations). Each cell shows consensus, then my figure, then the difference.
EUR million, EPS in EUR FY2026 FY2027 FY2028 Remark
Revenue 9,975 / 9,954 (-0.2%) 10,046 / 9,914 (-1.3%) 10,124 / 9,993 (-1.3%)
EBITDA before restructuring 1,928 / 1,937 (+0.4%) 1,933 / 1,909 (-1.2%) 1,968 / 1,914 (-2.7%) The median mixes bases; three brokers are far lower

  1. The direction is already set, and one cut is not yet in the numbers. Consensus FY2027 EPS has fallen 2.3% in a month and 12.7% in twelve months; FY2028 has fallen 2.6% and 23.0%. Bernstein (2026-11-09) expects low-to-mid single-digit downgrades to FY2027 consensus EPS. Morgan Stanley's 8% cut of 2026-11-12 came after the FactSet date. The three houses that have stated their FY2027 organic growth since the results (-1.0%, -1.0%, -1.2%) are below the five brokers still at +0.8% to +2.0% of reported growth. Those five have to come down when the guide is given, if not before.

  2. Line by line: which estimates are exposed, and when. - Revenue: a cut of about 1-1.5% for FY2027-FY2028 by the February results. High confidence. - EBITDA: a cut of about 1-3%. The margin part depends on the H1 2027 print. At about 13.6% (my input), against brokers whose stated full-year margins run from 13.9% to 14.5%, the direction is down or flat. - EPS: this must be read broker by broker, as the second set said and AlphaSense confirms. Deutsche Bank's EUR 14.33 and Redburn's EUR 14.35 for FY2026 exclude restructuring. Redburn models no restructuring cost at all in FY2027-FY2028. Bernstein's EUR 13.96 carries EUR 50 million of share-based payment. Morgan Stanley carries EUR 50 million a year of restructuring in its reported EPS. A restructuring guide of EUR 50-80 million therefore leaves "adjusted" EPS untouched, and cuts reported EPS and free cash flow where brokers assumed none. - Free cash flow: the most exposed line. Bernstein (EUR 913 million) and Redburn (EUR 879 million) for FY2027 are EUR 130-160 million above my figure. About half of the gap is restructuring cash, including an overhang from FY2026 (EUR 56 million was paid in H1 2026 against EUR 109 million charged); the rest is lower EBITDA and higher capital expenditure in my inputs. - Reported EPS and book value: exposed to an impairment on 2027-02-25, which no forecast contains. In FY2025 impairments caused a 13% miss on reported EPS.

  3. Breadth. The share of buy ratings is 20%, down 7 points in a month and 20 in six months. The dashboard's revision score is -11 and its letter F. Morgan Stanley has since moved to Underweight. Price targets have not followed estimates down (+1% over three months; mean EUR 77.58, median EUR 72.00, range EUR 45-105): they are the next thing to be cut. I see nothing before the February results that turns breadth positive. After them, breadth can stop falling if the guide is at or below consensus, because those who have been cutting will have less to add. An actual upturn needs upgrades, and the first candidates are (a) the FY2027 margin after an H1 2027 print of 14.0% or better, in late July 2027, and (b) FY2028 revenue after a positive Q3 2027, in November 2027.

  4. My probabilities for consensus FY2027 EPS (the median, as FactSet states it). Lower by the February results than today: about 80%. Lower again between February and July 2027: about 50%. Higher at the end of 2027 than after the February results: about 30%.

  5. 18 months to three years. Consensus for FY2028-FY2029 assumes revenue growth of about +0.8%, EBITDA margins rising to 19.4-19.6%, EPS growth of 3.4% and 4.5%, total debt flat at about EUR 4.97 billion and no buybacks. Against my inputs, revenue is in line by FY2029; the margin is about 0.3-0.6 point too high on the brokers' basis; and there is no credit for net debt falling by about EUR 1 billion by end-2029, or for EUR 500 million of buybacks in FY2029-FY2030. Net, I expect small cuts to FY2028 (about 2-4% for EPS on a like definition) and then a consensus that is held, with the balance sheet as the upside the sell-side has not modelled. A disposal would break every one of these numbers. On Bernstein's EUR 2.5 billion it removes about 30% of EBITA, and whether EPS rises or falls depends on a split of proceeds that Morgan Stanley, and the board's stated priority, both put mainly to debt.

D-0735
D-07

To what extent do the share price and the multiple move with sector and industry peers rather than idiosyncratically (on a rolling six-month basis), and so how far is the multiple company-driven rather than industry-driven?

F3545th of 55

Layer 3 · Risks

Judgement. Over the latest six months the share price has moved almost entirely on its own. The dashboard's peer basket explains 8% of its daily price variance (correlation 0.29); 92% is specific to the stock. The multiple is company-driven or, more exactly, driven by the company and by the narrower trade in the automation of customer service that it shares with Concentrix and TaskUs, which are not in the basket and whose co-movement I cannot measure from the files. The dependence is not constant. When fear of AI was marked down across business services as one group (October 2025 to February 2026), the basket explained 23-30% and the stock moved 1.6 to 2.2 times as far as the sector. So in calm conditions the multiple answers to the company's own news, and in a group-wide scare the stock is the sector's high-beta member.

Reasoning.

  1. The figures (co-movement table, 126 trading days to 2026-11-12). Correlation with the equal-weighted basket of 20 dashboard peers 0.29; beta 1.26; R-squared 8%. The highest single-peer readings are Serco (R-squared 8%), Compass (7%) and Verisure (6%); fifteen of the twenty are at 2% or less. Six-month return: Teleperformance 0.0%, the basket +5.3%.

  2. The history shows a change of regime in March 2026. R-squared was 14-24% (mostly 14-18%) from late 2024 to September 2025, rose to 23-30% from October 2025 to February 2026 with a beta of 1.6-2.2, then fell to 14% in March 2026 and to 4-11% since. March 2026 is when the founder stepped down, the new chief executive started and the chairman's swap was disclosed. From then the price has followed company events: the portfolio review, the H1 margin, the November guidance cut.

  3. The multiple confirms it. The price is up 52% over nine months and 28% over twelve, while consensus FY2027 EPS fell about 9-13% over twelve months. The whole gain, and more, was the multiple rising from about its five-year low (3.3 times) to 4.8 times. No sector move explains that. The dashboard's ratings show the same split: technical momentum A, sell-side earnings momentum F. Within the dashboard sector Teleperformance's multiple is at the first percentile of its own ten-year history; five other peers are in their bottom decile (Edenred, Green Landscaping, Rentokil, Pluxee, Verisure) and six are above their 60th percentile, each for its own reasons. The sector has no common cycle (first set, C-07), and the table bears that out.

  4. The dashboard basket is the wrong test of "industry". It holds caterers, security, facilities and voucher companies. The peers that share Teleperformance's driver are Concentrix and TaskUs (down 38% and 48% in the first months of 2026, per Morgan Stanley) and the IT-services names. The precedents say this narrower factor is strong on event days (Klarna's announcement of 2024-02-28; Meta's Muse in September 2026), and that the next Concentrix results, about mid-January 2027, are a read-across event. My conclusion that the multiple is company-driven therefore means: driven by the company and by news on the automation of customer service, not by the business-services sector.

  5. What this means for the case. - A re-rating of the sector will not lift this stock, and a sell-off of the sector in calm conditions will not sink it. - The multiple will respond to the company's own events, which are dated (D-05). This is why the reset is the pivot of the next six months. - A multiple that rose about 45% on a change of governance while estimates fell can give that back on one disappointment. The fall from about EUR 82 to EUR 69-71 on the guidance cut of 2026-11-05 is the scale. - In a renewed group-wide AI scare, plan on about twice the sector's move.

Why it is not placed It asks whether the stock fits the sector's historical patterns of share price and financial outperformance, a cross-check across many topics rather than one of the eleven.

C-84

What do historical precedents, rules of thumb and the empirical evidence show about how companies in this sector and industry have typically generated share price, financial and operational outperformance, and underperformance, and to what extent does this company fit those patterns?

F1253rd of 55

Layer 1 · Industry and Value Chain

Judgement. In this industry, outperformance has come from organic growth carried by outsourcing and offshoring penetration together with stable margins. Underperformance has come from guidance cuts in decelerating growth, labour scandals, disruption demonstrations, client-concentration shocks, and large late-cycle acquisitions followed by impairments. Teleperformance currently fits every element of the underperformance pattern and none of the outperformance pattern. The precedent most relevant for the next 18 months is that self-help and disposals do not re-rate a stock while estimates for its core are still falling.

Reasoning.

  1. Outperformance precedents. Teleperformance itself: organic growth of 8.0% a year from 2010 to 2019, +11.6% in 2020, margins of 12-15%, a price-to-earnings ratio near 30 times by late 2021. Accretive specialised bolt-ons (ZP at about 35% margin).
  2. Underperformance precedents. - The de-rating from 30 times to under 5 times (2021-2026), with FY2026 EPS cut 35% over three years. - November 2022: a labour exposé, -34% in a day. - 2024-02-28: Klarna's AI assistant, -14% in a day. - TaskUs: its top client cut revenue 22%. - Concentrix: Webhelp followed by USD 2.6 billion of impairments. - Teleperformance: Majorel followed by impairments elsewhere in the portfolio and synergies short of target.
  3. Rules of thumb from the Sector and Industry digests, and the fit. - "Clients claim most automation savings at renewal; the lasting gain is scale": fits, and is negative for Teleperformance's per-hour book. - "A fear marked down as one group clears company by company where results show little damage": not met; Q3 showed damage. - "A disruption de-rating clears from the highest-quality name outward" (Wisdom Library): Teleperformance is the transactional, labour-heavy end, so it would clear last. - "Where no bid comes, a buyback at a low multiple raises EPS every year": Teleperformance is not buying back. - "Private bids come for businesses a private owner can finance": fits LanguageLine and TLScontact, not the group, given its size, its 2.1 times leverage and the disruption.
  4. Analogue: Pernod Ricard. EUR 1.5 billion of disposals of lower-margin brands and a EUR 1 billion cost programme did not stop the de-rating (11.3 times against a ten-year average of 20.1 times) while consensus EPS kept falling (-22.4% year on year for FY2026). Teleperformance would be selling its higher-margin asset, which makes a re-rating less likely still.
  5. Wisdom Library: "Slow growth is punished twice", "Turnarounds take longer than you think", "Worser, odder, longer, further".

For the model. Do not model multiple expansion off self-help alone. The re-rating trigger in the precedents is organic stabilisation, observed over at least two quarters.

Why it is not placed The table holds this question in reserve for Riskiness sub-items that are not yet built, so it is not yet placed.

C-83

How far could the shares fall to a fair trough multiple?

D4530th of 55

Judgement. To a fair trough multiple on today's estimates the shares could fall to about EUR 50-56, 22-30% below EUR 71.3. If the market re-bases to clean earnings at the same trough multiple, the stress case is about EUR 42-45, about 40% lower. The dividend (EUR 4.50-4.55) is the main support: at EUR 52 it would yield about 8.7%.

Reasoning (my calculations from the FactSet block).

  1. The anchor. Today's 24-month-forward price-to-earnings ratio of 4.79 times at EUR 71.68 implies 24-month-forward EPS of about EUR 15.0.
