Appendix Research requests the Investment Analyst raised for the memo

Research requests for the next set

Company: Teleperformance SE (TEP-FR), the global outsourced customer-experience provider; it reports two segments: Core Services (customer care, technical support, back office, Trust & Safety content moderation, collections and data services for AI) and Specialized Services (LanguageLine interpretation, TLScontact visa services and Health Advocate).

Request: OWN-1

Question: How do investors, as distinct from sell-side analysts, currently describe and position themselves in Teleperformance SE (TEP-FR), in particular since the company cut its FY2026 like-for-like revenue guidance to -1.5% to -2.5% on 2026-11-05 and Morgan Stanley moved to Underweight on 2026-11-12? Cover fund letters and portfolio-manager commentary, questions asked by investors on results calls and at conferences, interviews and press coverage, statements by shareholders (including Saham Group, which built a stake of about 14.9% through an equity swap entered on 2026-03-02, and FIL), regulatory disclosures of holdings, short positions and derivative positions (AMF and other filings), insider transactions since August 2026, and publicly visible investor commentary on social media. For each of the following debates, say whether investors raise it prominently, rarely or not at all, with names and dates: when the drag from Trust & Safety on group growth ends; whether automation spreads from content moderation into voice customer care; whether restructuring charges are recurring and which earnings-per-share definition investors use to value the shares; when share buybacks resume against the 1.2 times leverage target; the value and use of proceeds of a disposal of LanguageLine or TLScontact; the risk of a goodwill impairment at the FY2026 results; and what investors expect from the FY2027 guidance and the revisit of the 2028 targets. Also report how the shareholder register and positioning changed during the share-price rise from February to October 2026 and after 2026-11-05, and how investors in Concentrix (CNXC), TaskUs (TASK) and Capgemini (CAP-FR) discuss the same debates.

Request: OWN-2

Question: For Teleperformance SE (TEP-FR), which trades at about five times forward earnings (FactSet, 2026-11-11) after four consecutive years of cuts to its revenue guidance, a change of chief executive on 2026-03-16 and a pending revisit of its 2028 targets, what do precedents show about the valuation trough and the path of re-rating for comparable companies? Take listed companies that went through a similar sequence (repeated guidance cuts, a new chief executive, a reset of mid-term targets, and a debate about disruption or structural decline), including Concentrix (CNXC) in 2023-2026, TaskUs, TTEC, TELUS Digital, Atento, Capita, Atos, Worldline, Pernod Ricard, the staffing companies (Adecco, Randstad) and any earlier labour-based outsourcers or IT-services companies hit by offshoring or automation. For each, give the forward price-to-earnings and EV/EBITDA multiples at the trough; the number of months between the last guidance cut or the reset of targets and the trough in consensus earnings per share; the share price, multiple and consensus earnings per share 3, 6, 12 and 24 months after the reset; whether a goodwill impairment, a dividend cut, a buyback, a disposal or a bid accompanied or followed the reset; and what triggered any re-rating (organic growth turning positive, a first guidance met or raised, capital returns, a transaction). State the range of multiples at which labour-based outsourcers have traded while organic revenue was flat or falling and estimates were still being cut, and the range after two or more quarters of stable or positive organic growth, and give Teleperformance's own forward multiple at comparable points in its history (2009-2010; November 2022; February 2024; February 2026).

Request: OWN-3

Question: How large an effect will delivery-currency moves and wage inflation in delivery hubs have on Teleperformance SE's (TEP-FR) recurring EBITA margin and earnings per share in FY2027 and FY2028? Set out the company's exposures by currency (Philippine peso, Indian rupee, Colombian peso, Egyptian pound, Mexican peso, US dollar) as shares of revenue and of costs, and its stated sensitivities, from the 2025 Universal Registration Document and the H1 2026 interim report; its hedging policy and the hedge book for 2027 (notional amounts, average hedged rates and maturities) compared with spot rates after the US dollar reached a record of about 64.20 Philippine pesos in late 2026 and the rupee fell about 7.5% against the dollar over twelve months (Morgan Stanley, 2026-10-27); the contract clauses that pass currency gains, cost-of-living increases or statutory wage increases through to clients, and what clients have demanded at renewal when delivery currencies weakened; the statutory wage increases scheduled or proposed for 2027 (the second Metro Manila step of PHP 25 a day in January 2027, the proposed nationwide Philippine increase, Colombia's 2027 minimum wage, India's Labour Codes, Egypt); what each broker assumes for the currency effect on FY2027 revenue and margin; and how earlier depreciations of the peso and the rupee (2022 and 2024-2025) showed up in the reported margins of Teleperformance, Concentrix, TaskUs, Ibex, Genpact and Firstsource, and with what delay.
